Here's the one-time gift as I understand it:

**One-time gift (parent's grace)**
- Parent creates it from the same simple screen as the allowance: amount, plus "one time" as the frequency choice, plus a date (defaults to today). Optional short note.
- It's a surprise. Until handed over, the child can't see it anywhere: no badge, no schedule entry, no hint. Enforced in the database, not just hidden in the UI.
- Parent gets the due badge and reminders from that date, same as allowance (first notice on the day, then every 3 days).
- Handover is the same screen as allowance: parent splits between Spend and Save, confirms, posts exactly once.
- After handover the child sees it in history with the note, marked as a gift, not allowance.
- *My guess:* it's recorded as its own kind of entry, separate from allowance and from FR15 emergency money, so weekly reviews and history can tell them apart.
- *My guess:* parent can edit or cancel it while it's still unconfirmed. Once handed over it's final, same as any confirmed entry.
- *My guess:* if parent hands over less than the gift amount, it's recorded as a shortfall the same way allowance is (D1). That said, for a gift it might be simpler to just record what was actually given.

What's wrong or missing there?

After that, the recurring side. The spec lets the child plan their own Spend/Save split, and that plan prefills the parent's handover screen. Do you want to keep that for the first version, or should the parent just type the split each time?

Reviewer came back with four questions. Three I can settle myself and will note as my calls:
- **Changing frequency or day:** the change screen reuses the setup screen, pre-filled, with a "Next one on" date that becomes the new starting point. That date must come after any allowance already created.
- **Past dates:** the first allowance date and a gift's "Give on" date must be today or later, so nothing gets backfilled.
- **Pause timing:** a pause starts the moment the parent taps it, with no booking ahead. Anything due before today stays due, even if the scheduler is running late. Anything already created stays due too, even if it falls due today.

One is yours, and it changes what gets built. Right now a due allowance can only be cleared by handing money over. So if a parent stops the allowance, or decides not to pay an old one, the due badge and reminders stay on forever.

Should the parent be able to mark a due allowance as "not given" so it closes without money, or should due allowances always wait for a handover?

The slice 2 design is done and ready for you to review: [docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-29-slice-2-allowance-handover-design.md](docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-29-slice-2-allowance-handover-design.md). It isn't committed yet.

"Not given" is in as decision S10. It closes a due allowance without adding any money, turns off the badge and reminders, and is recorded in the audit log.

These are calls I made myself, so please check them as you read:
- **Gifts get their own table**, separate from the allowance schedule, and their own entry type, so they never get mixed up with gifts the child records from other people or with emergency money.
- **Gift handover records what was actually given**, more or less than planned. There's no shortfall for gifts, and a cap of 100× the planned amount guards against typos.
- **Split prefill** uses the save share of the last handover; the very first handover goes all to Spend.
- **Notes and pause messages** are optional and limited to 140 characters.
- **Changing the allowance** reuses the setup screen, with a "Next one on" date that becomes the new start. Frequency and day can change without stopping and recreating it.
- **No past dates** for the first allowance date, the next date or a gift's "Give on" date.
- **Pause starts the moment the parent taps it.** Anything due before today, or already created, stays due.
- **"Not given" in the child's history** shows as a neutral "Skipped" with no amount.
- **The current docs change in this slice**: FR01 and FR03 in `REQUIREMENTS.md`, plus `docs/domain.md` and `docs/architecture.md`.

The mockup tab is still running at the same link if you want to look again. Once you approve the document, I'll commit it and write the implementation plan.

I closed the mock server and wrote the implementation plan: [docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-29-slice-2-allowance-handover.md](docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-29-slice-2-allowance-handover.md). The spec and the plan are committed on branch `docs/slice-2-allowance-design`; nothing is pushed yet.

**One change to the spec while planning:** pause, resume and stop now first bring the allowance up to date to today. So anything due up to and including the pause day stays due, and the database doesn't need to store pause windows. This is simpler than the rule you saw.

The plan has 12 tasks:
- **Task 0:** update the requirements and architecture docs.
- **Task 1:** money parsing and formatting helpers.
- **Tasks 2–6:** database work, each with its own tests:
  - the ledger and balances
  - allowance schedules with pause and stop
  - the hourly scheduler
  - handover and "not given"
  - hidden gifts
- **Task 7:** the server code behind the screens.
- **Tasks 8–10:** the parent setup, handover and child home screens, each with end-to-end tests.
- **Task 11:** a test that fires several handover confirmations at once, the full checks, and the reviewer agents.

The plan also lists five risks to watch:
- a gift leaking to the child some other way, such as through totals or counts
- allowances set on the 29th–31st landing on the wrong date
- money typed in odd formats
- double taps on Confirm
- allowances becoming due at the wrong time near midnight in the family's timezone

Please review the plan. How should we run it?
- **Subagent-driven:** a fresh agent builds each task and a fresh reviewer checks it before the next one starts. Most thorough, but costs more.
- **Native:** I build every task in this session, then one reviewer checks the whole branch at the end. Cheaper and faster.

I recommend subagent-driven. Tasks 2–6 each define names the later tasks depend on, and a money or gift-secrecy mistake reaching the app costs more than the extra reviews.

Slice 2 is built and reviewed on branch `feat/allowance-handover`: 38 commits, rebased onto the latest `origin/main`. Nothing is pushed or merged yet.

**Tests on the final tree:** 302 unit tests and 381 database tests pass. Lint, typecheck, the production build and all 24 end-to-end tests also passed on the last code commit; only the session log was committed after that.

**What's in it:**
- **Parent:** sets up an allowance, changes, pauses, resumes or stops it, and gives one-time gifts that stay hidden until handed over.
- **Handover:** one screen with the Spend/Save split, plus "mark as not given". A test fires several confirms at once and only one posts.
- **Jars and history:** shown to the parent, and to the child on their home screen.
- **Scheduler:** creates due allowances every hour, in the family's timezone.
- **Reviews:** a security and child-safety audit found no way for a pending gift to reach the child. Two other final reviews found problems that are now fixed, including "5,50" in a euro family being read as €550.

**Left open (small):**
- The slice 2 spec still has an old line about an app-side scheduler backup route, which now contradicts the new note; it should be deleted.
- Indian grouping like "1,00,000" is rejected with a friendly error, so INR parents have to type 100000.
- On an allowance paused until a date, changing only the amount can move a month-end anchor (31st → 28th).
- **Before release:** pg_cron must be confirmed enabled on the hosted Supabase project.

**Decisions I made along the way (each is cheap to reverse):**
1. Docs, money helpers and the ledger were built in parallel, then rebased onto `origin/main`, which had two commits your local `main` didn't.
2. "Next one" for a pause with an end date is the first period after the pause; with no end date it is empty.
3. An allowance or change dated today is due straight away, not at the next hourly run.
4. Pausing an already-paused allowance is refused. To change the end date, resume then pause again.
5. The ledger's duplicate-protection keys come from the allowance or gift row, not the browser.
6. A gift that is already due can still be edited without changing its date.
7. History shows the date money was handed over, not the due date.
8. Cancelling a gift asks for confirmation.
9. A gift handover may be more than planned, up to 100×, as the spec allows.
10. On the first handover the hint reads "Drag or type to change." without "same split as last time".
11. One fix commit accidentally included Task 10's unfinished test file; I left the history as is.
12. Parent jars and history were built now rather than dropped from scope.
13. The child can't read gift rows at all; their history comes only from the ledger.
14. Lock order for money writes is the allowance or gift row first, then the child's ledger lock. Slices 3–4 must follow the same order.
15. A cancelled or not-given item shows "Already handled." when someone tries to confirm it.
16. The app-side scheduler backup route is postponed to slice 5.

What would you like to do?

1. Merge back to `main` locally
2. Push and create a Pull Request
3. Keep the branch as-is (I'll handle it later)

Which option?

I'd split the parent side into three levels: **Home** shows what needs you today, a **child page** is one screen per child, and **Settings** holds family admin.

## Why it feels cluttered

The parent side has only one tab, so everything lands on Home, and the allowance page stacks every allowance feature on one long scroll.

**Parent Home today** ([parent/page.tsx](src/app/(app)/parent/page.tsx)):
- The family code card sits at the top, but that's a one-time setup thing and it's already on the Settings page in your screenshot.
- Each child row has an Allowance button with a due count, plus Set/Reset PIN. PIN reset is now also in Settings, so it appears twice.
- Below that are Add a child and Accept an invitation.
- Missing: anything that answers "what do I need to do now?" Due handovers are hidden behind a small count on each child's button.

**Allowance page today** ([allowance/page.tsx](src/app/(app)/parent/children/[childId]/allowance/page.tsx)) stacks seven blocks in a row:
1. Due list
2. Allowance summary with change, pause and stop
3. Upcoming gifts
4. "Give a one-time gift" button
5. Jars
6. The full history
7. The setup form, in place of the summary when nothing is set up

That one page mixes three different jobs: acting now, managing settings, and looking back.

**Navigation:** [parent-nav.tsx](src/components/shell/parent-nav.tsx) has only "Home". [docs/design.md §5](docs/design.md) planned **Home · Requests · Allowance · Inbox**, and §236 says "Home opens on what needs them: due handovers, pending requests, limit notices, each with its action inline." The build hasn't caught up with that plan yet.

## What I found

- **Greenlight** gives each child their own area, with tabs inside it. Chores are reviewed "within each child's Chores tab" ([Greenlight help](https://help.greenlight.com/hc/en-us/articles/360019317134-How-can-my-children-see-their-chores-list-)). Allowance setup (how often, which day, the split) is its own area, not on the home screen ([Bankrate](https://www.bankrate.com/personal-finance/family-apps-to-manage-allowances-and-chores)).
- **GoHenry** puts allowance setup (payment day, amount, recurring) under Settings rather than Home ([GoHenry blog](https://www.gohenry.com/us/blog/parental-controls/what-to-expect-when-getting-started-with-gohenry)). Its home screen centres on balances and quick actions like Transfer ([Nutsaboutmoney](https://www.nutsaboutmoney.com/visit/gohenry)).
- **Bottom tab bars** should hold 3–5 core, frequently used destinations, always with labels; everything else goes behind a menu ([UX Planet](https://uxplanet.org/bottom-tab-bar-navigation-design-best-practices-48d46a3b0c36), [Bootcamp](https://bootcamp.uxdesign.cc/bottom-tab-bar-design-best-practices-navigating-user-experience-with-finesse-4dc63420f35d)).

I couldn't find official screen-by-screen layouts for either app, only help articles and reviews, so treat those two as general patterns rather than exact copies.

The shared pattern: **Home = act now**, **child page = one child at a glance**, **allowance setup = its own place**, and admin stays in Settings.

## Proposed layout

**1. Parent Home: "what needs you"**
- **Needs you:** every due handover and gift for every child, each with a "Hand over" button, oldest first. When nothing is due, one quiet line: "All caught up."
- **Children:** one compact card per child with nickname, Spend and Save balances, and "Next: ₹500 on Sun 2 Nov" (or Paused). Tapping a card opens the child page.
- **Moved off Home:** the family code card and PIN reset (both already in Settings). "Add a child" and "Accept an invitation" become a single small link under the children list.

**2. Child page** at `/parent/children/[id]`, a new hub for one child:
- Jars at the top (Spend and Save).
- That child's due items, if there are any.
- An allowance card with one line ("₹500 every Sunday · Next: 9 Nov") and a **Manage** button.
- Upcoming gifts, plus "Give a gift".
- Recent history (last 5 entries) with "See all".

**3. Allowance management** at `/parent/children/[id]/allowance`. This page shrinks to just the summary, Change, Pause/Resume and Stop, plus the setup form when nothing is set up. Due items, jars and history move off it.

**4. Full history** gets its own page.

**5. Tab bar:** keep Home only for now. An **Allowance** tab listing every child's schedule only pays off with several children, and Requests and Inbox arrive in slices 4–5. Adding tabs now would mean tabs that lead nowhere. The settings cog stays in the header as it is.

This is mostly moving existing components between pages. The only new pieces are the "Needs you" list across all children (built on the existing per-child due lists) and the compact child card. The end-to-end tests for allowance and child home will need updating, because what they look for moves to different pages.

I can show this as mockups in the browser before building it. Do you want the Allowance tab added now, or kept back until there are requests and an inbox to sit beside it?

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. **Primary Request and Intent**
   - Brainstorm, then build, FR01/FR03 slice 2 (allowance, gifts, handover) of Jarso, a pocket-money app for children aged 11–14 (Next.js 16 plus Supabase).
   - User decisions:
     - simple setup (amount, frequency, "One time");
     - one-time gift is parent-initiated, can be scheduled, is hidden from the child until handover, has an optional note;
     - the parent decides the Spend/Save split (child split comes later);
     - practice money is deferred to the learning section;
     - pause/stop are allowed and the child sees "Paused";
     - the parent can mark an allowance "not given".
   - Run independent tasks in parallel.
   - Refactor the cluttered parent UI after research and mockups. The user chose option **B: Home · Allowance tabs**.
   - Theme: "no need for system, so a toggle will be good instead of a modal opening, one click."
   - "raise the PR", then fix the CI secrets failure.
   - Spawn subagents to resolve CodeRabbit comments on #18 and #20.
   - Latest: "resolve the conflicts: https://github.com/harisshakeel/Jarso/pull/18 and PR #20 is merged".

2. **Key Technical Concepts**
   - Next.js 16 App Router with Cache Components (Suspense for request-time reads), shadcn/ui on Base UI, Tailwind v4, Zod, `createAction`, Vitest, Playwright, Biome.
   - Supabase Postgres: RLS with per-operation policies; `security definer set search_path = ''`; pgTAP via `supabase test db`; pg_cron hourly job `generate_due_occurrences`.

     - per-child advisory lock taken after the source row lock;

CodeRabbit left 3 new comments on #18. I fixed all three, replied on each thread and marked them resolved. The fixes are pushed (head `3132bd2`); CI has not run on them yet.

1. **Doc mismatch (minor):** the spec and plan said a repeat handover with the same key returns the original result. They now describe what the database actually does: the row lock is what prevents a double handover, the key isn't stored, and any call on a row that is no longer due returns `already_confirmed`. Commit `653efbc`.

2. **History stopped at 50 entries (major):** the parent's history page couldn't show anything older than the newest 50. It now has "Show older" and "Back to newest" links. This needed a new migration, `20261029130006_ledger_history_before.sql`, which adds a cursor to the ledger history query. A handover's two rows (Spend and Save) are never split across pages. The child page and the parent overview still show only the newest entries. Commit `6615aad`.

3. **Flaky e2e risk (minor):** `tests/e2e/allowance.spec.ts` was generating due allowances for every schedule in the database. Specs run in parallel, so it could change another spec's data. It now generates only for its own child's schedule. Commit `81b9b48`.

Locally everything passes: the checks (353 unit tests), the build, the database tests and all 31 e2e tests. There's no e2e test that loads more than 50 entries; the paging logic is covered by unit tests and one new database test.

The new migration means one more file goes to the hosted database when you run `pnpm db:push` after merging.

The hosted Supabase project hasn't taken any schema changes since #17 merged. I've opened [PR #21](https://github.com/harisshakeel/Jarso/pull/21) to fix it, and after merging you'll need to run four commands once by hand.

**What broke:** Supabase's GitHub integration fails before it gets to migrations, and it has failed the same way on every merge since #20. It also applies our `config.toml`, which sets a custom sign-up confirmation email. Free-tier projects using Supabase's built-in email sender aren't allowed to change email templates ([Supabase changelog](https://supabase.com/changelog/46599-changes-to-email-template-customisation-on-free-tier)), so that step is rejected.

As a result, the live database is missing #17's `120000_parent_details` migration and our two newest ones (`130006`, `130007`). The live app code from #17 probably needs those parent profile columns, so expect parent pages to be broken until this is fixed.

**A second problem:** before the rename, you had already pushed slice 2 to hosted under its old names (`2026102911000x`). Hosted therefore records version numbers the repo no longer has. I pulled what hosted actually ran: four of the five files are identical, and only the ledger file is missing the CodeRabbit fix (keeping a child's ledger history when the child is removed).

**What PR #21 changes:**
- **`config.toml`:** the custom confirmation email section is commented out until a custom email provider (SMTP) is set up. Local sign-up doesn't send confirmation emails, so nothing local uses it. `docs/auth.md` says when to turn it back on.
- **`130001_ledger.sql`:** now identical to what hosted ran.
- **`130007_ledger_keep_child_history.sql` (new):** carries the ledger fix. A fresh database ends up with the same schema as before.
- **Tests:** unit (353), database (439) and e2e (31/31) all pass. The first e2e run after I restarted the local stack had failures; the next two runs passed 31/31.

**After merging, run this once from `main`** (I'm not allowed to push to production):
```bash
supabase migration repair --status reverted 20261029110001 20261029110002 20261029110003 20261029110004 20261029110005
supabase migration repair --status applied 20261029130001 20261029130002 20261029130003 20261029130004 20261029130005
supabase db push --include-all     # applies 120000, 130006, 130007
supabase migration list --linked   # every row should show both local and remote
```
- The two `repair` lines only change the list of which migrations hosted has recorded; they don't touch tables. They tell hosted to forget the five old names and record the new ones as already done.
- `--include-all` is needed because `120000` is older than versions hosted already records as applied.
- Check that pg_cron is enabled and the `generate-due-occurrences` job exists. Hosted already ran the slice 2 migration that creates that job, so it should be there.

Until custom SMTP is set up, hosted sends Supabase's default confirmation email. Its link only works in the browser that started the sign-up. Email confirmation is off for now anyway, so nobody is affected yet.

GitHub created the PR with the "Generated with Claude Code" line from my session instructions. Your rules forbid that, so I removed it from the PR body.

It's an old failure, not a new problem. That check ran once, at 14:50 UTC, when #21 merged, and hasn't run since, so it still shows red.

**Why it failed then:** Supabase compares the database's list of applied migrations with the files on `main` before it applies anything. At 14:50 the hosted database still had the five old `2026102911000x` names, which don't exist in the repo any more. That is the "Remote migration versions not found in local migrations directory" error, and the integration stopped there.

**Why it's fine now:** your repair removed those five old names. I just re-checked against current `main` (`5d9e7a0`): all 17 migrations are listed on both the repo side and the hosted side, with no mismatches.

That run did get past the email template rejection that failed the two earlier runs (#18 and #20), so the `config.toml` fix works.

To clear the red check, it just needs to run again:
- **Re-run it in Supabase:** Project Settings → Integrations → GitHub. GitHub won't let me re-run a check owned by Supabase's app.
- **Or wait for the next merge to `main`:** it runs then, and with nothing new to apply it should pass.

I can also push an empty commit to `main` to trigger it now, if you're OK with a direct push to `main`.

The cron check from before is still open: please run `select jobname, schedule, active from cron.job;` in the dashboard SQL editor. I was blocked from querying production myself.

Report only the questions you'd still need answered before you could plan this well: places where you'd have to guess and a wrong guess would matter. For each, give the question and why the answer changes the plan. Rank them by importance.

Skip anything the document explicitly leaves to the builder, and anything you could reasonably decide yourself. Skip nitpicks about wording.

If nothing important is left, say "nothing important left".


[CLAUDE_LOG_ENTRY type=SUBAGENT_RESPONSE num=1 session=3336f19b-eaaf-4cb6-a13d-6ae3f17c0076 agent=aec3fd0dfc125f0c1]
timestamp: 2026-10-29T08:32:47.983Z
time: 2026-10-29 08:32
chars: 3510

I read the slice 2 spec, the milestone spec, REQUIREMENTS FR01/FR03, architecture §5–§9 and the current migrations. No allowance, ledger or gift tables exist yet, `families.timezone` already exists, and there is no `clock_offset`. Four questions are still open, most important first.

1. **What does "Change amount or how often" do to the dates?**
   - D6 says a change applies from the next occurrence that hasn't been generated. That is enough for an amount change, but not for a frequency change.
   - Take a parent who moves weekly (Sundays from 2 Nov) to monthly. Which date is the first monthly occurrence? It could be the old `starts_on` stepped monthly, the day after the last generated period, or a new date the parent picks.
   - Can the parent also move the weekday or day of month without stopping and recreating the allowance?
   - Does this screen reuse the setup screen with its "First one on" field, or show only amount and chips?
   - Why it matters: if the date can change, the schedule needs a re-anchor date. Without one, every change keeps stepping from `starts_on`. The answer also decides the change screen and the period-key and next-date logic and its tests. A wrong guess means occurrences on dates the parent didn't expect.

2. **Can a parent ever clear a due allowance without handing money over?**
   - `closed` is unused, D5 says nothing expires, and S7/S8 say "already due stays due".
   - So after a stop, or a long gap, old occurrences stay due for good. The badge stays on, and slice 5 sends a reminder every 3 days, with no way to clear them except a fake handover of at least 1 minor unit.
   - Is that intended for the pilot, or should stop (or a per-occurrence action) offer "close without handover" using `closed`?
   - Why it matters: it adds or removes a parent function, a state change, audit and history copy ("not given"), and a balance invariant test. It is hard to add later once reminders are built.

3. **Can a first date or "Give on" date be in the past?**
   - If a back-dated `starts_on` is allowed, the first generator run creates every past period as a separate due occurrence. A yearly allowance started in 2024 would create several of them at once.
   - Why it matters: it decides Zod and DB validation (today or later only, or a limit on how far back), the generator's catch-up behaviour, and the summary-line copy. For gifts, a past `give_on` makes the gift due at once. That's harmless, but it should be decided together with this.

4. **When does a pause start, and is a period due on the pause day inside the pause?**
   - Does a pause always start the moment the parent taps "Pause", or can it be booked ahead ("pause over the holiday next month")? A booked pause needs a stored start date and changes how the pause window is modelled.
   - Does "already due before the pause" mean due by date (`due_on` before the pause started) or already generated? The generator runs hourly and can run late, so the two can differ.
   - Is a period due on the day the parent pauses skipped or kept?
   - Why it matters: these are the exact edges the "late generator across a pause" tests check. Since §4.1 leaves the pause storage to the builder, the start-date rule decides which design is correct.

The other gaps I found are covered by builder's-call items or I can decide them myself: single vs per-source handover function, `child_settings` timing, the prefill basis when the last handover had a shortfall, and hiding a gift's planned amount from the child.

Too many fraction digits gives null, and so does a result that is not a safe integer. Symbols are collected from `Intl` currency parts (symbol and narrowSymbol) across en, en-GB, en-US, en-IN, en-AU, en-CA and ja-JP. They are removed longest first, so "A$" goes before "$".
- Edge cases: ".5", "5." and currency codes such as "GBP 5" return null.
- I added a few tests beyond the brief: "A$5" for AUD, "£5.25" for GBP, a whole amount with default "always" giving ".00", and cents kept under "nonzero".

1. Spec compliance: ✅
- The signatures of `minorDigits`, `formatMoney` and `parseMoney` match the brief and are exported via `src/lib/index.ts:25`.
- `parseMoney` converts on the string (`src/lib/money.ts:159`, `whole + fraction.padEnd(...)`), so no float touches a stored amount. It returns null for negative, too-precise, empty and non-numeric input.
- It strips whitespace, currency symbols and commas.
- All brief test cases are present in `src/lib/money.test.ts`. The tests also cover `A$` and `£`, and `formatMoney` with `showMinor: "nonzero"` on a non-whole amount.
- `formatMoney` does `minor / factor` as a float, but only for display, which fits the constraint "display only through `formatMoney`".
- I did not run the suite.

2. Quality: Approved, with minor items only.
- Minor, `money.ts:151`: stripping every comma means a decimal comma parses wrongly. `parseMoney("5,50", "EUR")` gives 55000, not 550. That is 100 times too large and no error is raised. The brief says to strip thousands commas, so this is compliant. Slice 2 is a GBP/INR-first app, but EUR is supported and a child or parent in a comma-decimal locale could hit it. Safer option: strip commas only when they match `^\d{1,3}(,\d{3})*(\.\d+)?$`, otherwise return null.
- Minor, `money.ts:151`: `[\s, ]` has a redundant space, since `\s` already covers it. Use `/[\s,]/g`.
- Minor, `money.ts:150`: symbols are removed from anywhere in the string, so `"5₹5"` parses as 55. Anchoring to a leading or trailing symbol is stricter.
- Minor, `money.ts:126-142`: `currencySymbols` builds 14 `Intl.NumberFormat` objects on every `parseMoney` call. Memoize per currency in a `Map`, since it will run on form input.
- Minor, `money.test.ts`: add a few edge cases. Examples are `"5."`, `".5"`, `"1e3"` and an oversized value (the `isSafeInteger` null path). Also add a `showMinor: "nonzero"` case for GBP, and a symbol-strip case for EUR or USD.
- Minor, `money.ts:97`: `ja-JP` and `en-IN` are in `SYMBOL_LOCALES`, but nothing checks that every supported currency's symbol is stripped. `"US$5"` for USD depends on `en-GB` producing `US$`, which is likely but not asserted.

3. ⚠️ Cannot verify from diff
- Whether Biome passes (formatting and lint), including the long line at `money.test.ts:81`.
- The exact `Intl` output depends on the ICU version in Node, for example `JP¥` against `¥` and `A$`. The tests assert `A$5`, and `£5.25` for GBP, so they could be environment-sensitive. I did not run them.

## Task 3 review: allowance schedules (3000dc5..97b60bd)

**1. Spec compliance: ✅**
- The table columns, checks, partial unique index, `unique(schedule_id, period_key)` and select-only RLS match the brief (migration :10-89).
- `allowance_due_on` always steps from the anchor with `make_interval`, never from the previous date (:108-120). Tests cover every clamp case the brief lists.
- `generate_for_schedule` is internal: revoked from public, anon and authenticated (:479-482). It locks with `for update`, only generates for active schedules, inserts with `on conflict do nothing` using the snapshot amount, and has the `-- slice 5: notify(due)` comment (:128-173).
- `create_allowance` takes `family_id` from the child's approved membership, never from input (:251-258). It raises JA403, 22023 and JA409, and a concurrent create that hits the unique index also gets JA409 (:272).
- Change, pause and stop catch up to today before mutating. Change requires `p_next_on >= today` and `> max(due_on)`, then re-anchors with `next_index = 0`, which satisfies D6 (:319-335). Pause trims the message, stores null when empty and caps it at 140 (:363, :374).
- Resume moves `next_index` to the first period due `>= today`, then generates (:420-432). Pause day P and resume day R: P's period is generated at pause, periods strictly between P and R are skipped, and R's period is generated. No off-by-one.
- Every function runs `security definer set search_path = ''` and uses `pg_catalog.`-qualified names. Grants follow the ledger migration's pattern. Each mutation writes one audit row.

**Concurrency:** Every parent function takes the row lock in `lock_allowance_for_parent` before catch-up. `generate_for_schedule` locks the same row again in the same transaction, which is harmless. A failed check after catch-up rolls back the generated rows. Concurrent creates are serialised by the partial unique index. No deadlock path found.

**Access:** A child or another family's parent gets JA403 from every write and from `allowance_next_due_on` (`is_parent_of` requires both the parent and the child to be approved in the same family). Tables give no write access to anon or authenticated, and select is limited to the child or their parent. `family_today` is security invoker, so a caller who can't see the family gets null; inside definer functions it runs as the owner, so it resolves there. No path for a child or another family to reach a function or row.

**2. Quality: Approved, with Minor items**
- **Minor:** `docs/database.md` (diff line 51) says each parent function "derives `family_id` from the child's approved membership". Only `create_allowance` does; change, pause, resume and stop use `allowance_schedules.family_id`. That is correct behaviour, since `family_members.profile_id` is unique. Fix: reword to "create derives family_id from the child's approved membership; the others use the schedule's".
- **Minor:** pausing an already-paused schedule (migration :366) re-pauses silently. It overwrites `paused_through` and the message and writes a second `allowance.paused` row. Either raise JA409 when the status is not `active`, or document re-pause as the way to edit a pause.
- **Minor:** create or change with a date of today does not generate that occurrence until the hourly job runs, while resume generates at once. Fix: call `generate_for_schedule(id, today)` at the end of create and change.
- **Minor:** tests use only UTC families (test :28-40). Add one non-UTC family so the timezone path of `family_today` is actually exercised.

**3. ⚠️ Cannot verify from diff**
- Whether the spec (S6/S7) expects a today-dated create or change to be due immediately; this decides the third Minor item.
- **Task 4 risks:**
  - Once a child's membership is `removed`, the parent gets JA403 on stop, so the schedule stays `active`. The generator job must skip children who are not approved.
  - Auto-resume after `paused_through` must skip forward the same way `resume_allowance` does (:420-423), as the implementer notes.
  - For a paused schedule, `allowance_next_due_on` can return a date inside the pause. The S9 child view needs the first period after `paused_through`.
- I did not re-run the test suite. `plan(44)` matches the 44 assertions the report claims.

1. **allowance_next_due_on: ADDRESSED.** The fix is in `supabase/migrations/20261029085518_allowance_schedules.sql`, in the new body of `allowance_next_due_on`.
   - A stopped schedule, or a paused one with null `paused_through`, returns null.
   - A paused schedule with `paused_through` loops from `next_index` while `due <= paused_through`, then returns that first later date.
   - The loop terminates because due dates strictly increase with the index for weekly, monthly, quarterly and annual.
   - Tests added: null when paused until resumed, and `today+14` for a pause through `today+10`. I traced the second by hand: create generates index 0, so `next_index` is 1, index 1 falls on `today+7` (skipped) and index 2 on `today+14` (returned).

2. **create/change generate today: ADDRESSED for create, partly tested for change.**
   - `create_allowance` calls `generate_for_schedule(v_id, family_today(v_family_id))` after the audit insert.
   - `change_allowance` calls `generate_for_schedule(v_s.id, v_today)` after its audit insert.
   - Only the create path has a test, "first date of today is due immediately". Nothing exercises change with `next_on = today`.
   - The test seeds that reset `next_index = 0` are correct and match the new behaviour.

3. **pause_allowance JA409 unless active: ADDRESSED.** It now raises on `status <> 'active'`. The test is `pause refused unless active`, run against the already-paused s4.

4. **docs/database.md wording: ADDRESSED.** The create and change/pause/stop wording is split as asked, and the docs also describe the new pause and next-due behaviour.

5. **Non-UTC family test: ADDRESSED.** Family B is now `INR` / `Asia/Kolkata`. Two `family_today(fb, ts)` assertions at 18:31 and 18:29 UTC check the day boundary. The plan count went from 44 to 50, which matches the 6 added assertions (the `lives_ok` swapped for an `is` doesn't change it).

**New breakage from the fix: none Critical or Important.**
- **Generate in create:** it runs after the audit insert, inside the same transaction. The function takes `for update` on the schedule row it just inserted, so there's no lock-ordering issue. It is internal and already called from other definer functions.
- **Generate in change:** it runs after the update and is a no-op for a paused schedule.
- **Next-due loop:** bounded by the `paused_through` distance. See the first observation below.

**Out-of-scope observations**
- `paused_through` has no visible upper bound, so a far-future date makes `allowance_next_due_on` loop once per period. Weekly through year 9999 is about 400k iterations, and it could hit date overflow. A closed-form index or a cap on the pause date would avoid that.
- Add a change-with-`next_on = today` test. The case is reachable when no generated `due_on` is at or after today.

1. Spec compliance: ✅
- Auto-resume (migration:22-47 of the new file, `generate_due_occurrences`): the skip loop `due <= paused_through` and the trigger `paused_through < today` agree on inclusive-through semantics. Periods in `(paused_through, today]` are generated, and pause fields are cleared. The test covers this with through=11-10 and run 11-20, giving 11-15 only.
- Child skip: the `exists` on `family_members` (role child, status approved, same family) matches the requirement.
- Per-schedule exception block: `v_failed` and `v_errors` are recorded, and the run ends as `error`. `details` carries `inserted`, `failed` and `errors`.
- Constraints: the function is `security definer set search_path = ''`, execute is revoked from public, anon and authenticated, only `p_now` is a time parameter, and the family-today expression is delegated to `family_today(fam, p_now)`. Exactly-once comes from `generate_for_schedule` (`on conflict do nothing`), and the `-- slice 5: notify(due)` comment is already there (Task 3 migration:162).

2. Quality: Approved. Minor issues only.

Focus areas, traced:
- **Overlapping runs:** run B hits `for update skip locked` on a schedule A holds and skips it. A concurrent run therefore cannot double-insert, and the unique key backstops it anyway. The row is re-read after the lock is taken (new statement snapshot), so a pause, resume or stop committed after the id list was built is seen. The nested `for update` in `generate_for_schedule` is a no-op because it is the same transaction.
- **Parent pause/resume vs generator:** these serialize on the row lock. A parent action blocks until the run commits, or the generator skips that schedule. Neither produces torn state.
- **Off-by-one at `paused_through`:** manual resume (`due < today`) and auto-resume (`due <= paused_through`) differ in form but are consistent. Both never pay periods inside the pause, and a period due exactly on the resume day is paid.
- **Timezone:** `p_now` goes through `family_today` per family, and the tests cover the 18:29 and 18:31 UTC Kolkata boundary. Half-hour offset zones are picked up at the next `:05`, a delay of up to about an hour, which is acceptable.
- **Partial state:** the `begin ... exception` block is a subtransaction. The status update, the audit insert and the occurrence inserts all roll back together on a failure, and `v_inserted` is only incremented on success. `continue` inside the block is valid plpgsql.

Minor:
- **Minor (migration:~34-36):** if `family_today` returns null, then `continue when paused_through >= null` evaluates to null and does not continue. The resume would then proceed with a null `v_today`. It is unreachable given the foreign key. Fix: `continue when v_today is null or ...`, or have the guard before it check `v_today is null`.
- **Minor:** the whole run is one transaction, so row locks on every generated schedule are held until the final `job_runs` update. A parent's pause or stop can wait for the entire run. At about 60 users this is negligible.
- **Minor:** the error record keeps only `sqlstate` and `schedule_id`, with no `sqlerrm`. That is arguably safer for a public repo, but harder to debug.
- **Minor (tests):** none of these paths is covered: the failure path (`failed` > 0, status `error`), `skip locked`, monthly month-end clamping, or a pause spanning many periods. The failure path is the only one that matters much. Fix: add a test that forces an error, for example a stub or a bad `frequency` row if the constraints permit.
- **Minor:** the membership check runs before the lock and is not repeated after it. A child removed in that window gets one extra generation, which is harmless.

3. ⚠️ Cannot verify from the diff:
- Whether `audit_log.actor_id` is nullable. The auto-resume insert uses a null actor.
- Whether `family_today` (security invoker) bypasses RLS when called from the definer function. This depends on the owner role having BYPASSRLS.
- Whether `service_role` keeps execute through Supabase default privileges, and whether `cron.schedule` with a repeated name upserts on the pg_cron version in use.
- Whether the pgTAP tests pass. I did not run them, per instruction.

The row is re-read after the lock is taken, with a new statement snapshot, so a pause, resume or stop that committed after the id list was built is seen. The nested `for update` in `generate_for_schedule` is a no-op because it runs in the same transaction.
- **Parent pause/resume vs generator:** these serialize on the row lock. A parent action blocks until the run commits, or the generator skips that schedule. I found no torn state.
- **Off-by-one at `paused_through`:** manual resume uses `due < today` and auto-resume uses `due <= paused_through`. They are consistent. Neither pays periods inside the pause, and both pay a period due exactly on the resume day.
- **Timezone:** `p_now` goes through `family_today` per family. The tests cover the Kolkata boundary at 18:29 and 18:31 UTC. Half-hour offset zones are picked up at the next `:05`, a delay of up to about an hour, which is acceptable.
- **Partial state:** the `begin ... exception` block is a subtransaction. The status update, audit insert and occurrence inserts roll back together on failure. `v_inserted` is incremented only on success. `continue` inside the block is valid plpgsql.

Minor:
- **Minor (paused branch, `continue when ... paused_through >= v_today`):** if `family_today` returned null, `continue when` would not fire and the resume would run with a null today. This is unreachable because of the foreign key. Fix: `continue when v_today is null or ...`.
- **Minor:** the whole run is one transaction, so row locks stay held until the final `job_runs` update. A parent's pause or stop can wait for the whole run. This is negligible at about 60 users.
- **Minor:** the error record keeps only `sqlstate` and `schedule_id`, with no `sqlerrm`. That is safe for a public repo but harder to debug.
- **Minor (tests):** these paths are not covered: the failure path (failed > 0, status `error`), `skip locked`, month-end clamping on a monthly schedule, and a pause spanning many periods. The failure path matters most. Fix: add a test that forces an error in one schedule while another still generates.
- **Minor:** the membership check happens before the lock and is not repeated after it. A child removed in that window gets one extra generation, which is harmless.

3. ⚠️ Cannot verify from the diff:
- Whether `audit_log.actor_id` is nullable. The auto-resume insert uses a null actor.
- Whether `family_today` (security invoker) bypasses RLS when called from the definer function. This depends on the owner role having BYPASSRLS.
- Whether service_role keeps execute through Supabase default privileges, and whether `cron.schedule` with a repeated name upserts on the pg_cron version in use.
- Whether the pgTAP tests pass. I did not run them, per instruction.

## Task 5 review: allowance handover and not given (80303a8..4e6c7e7)

**1. Spec compliance: ✅** (two small notes)
- Both functions are `security definer set search_path = ''`. Each locks the row with `for update` through `lock_occurrence_for_parent`, checks `is_parent_of`, and only changes a row that is `'due'`. Ledger rows take `family_id`/`child_id` from the locked row (migration:155,165). The amount checks match D1 and raise 22023 (migration:141-147). Shortfall is computed correctly (:174). The notify comments are present (:179, :215). `mark_not_given` posts no ledger rows and writes `audit_log('allowance.not_given')` (:209-213). Grants go to `authenticated` only, and the internal helper is revoked (:223-233).
- Note: spec §4.3 says a repeat call with the same key "returns the original result". The code returns `already_confirmed`, as the brief requires, and the UI treats that as success. This is a brief-vs-spec wording gap, not a defect.
- Note: the migration header (migration:95) cites "§3 S11-S12 and §4.2". It should say §4.3 / S10.

**2. Quality: Approved with issues**

Focus checks:
- **Race under READ COMMITTED: OK.** A second caller waiting on `select … for update` (migration:110) re-reads the newest committed row once the lock is released. It sees `confirmed`/`closed` and returns `already_confirmed`/`already_handled`, whatever key it used. Confirm racing not-given also gives exactly one outcome. The helper is declared `volatile`, which is correct: a `stable` declaration would be wrong here.
- **Authorisation order: OK.** Lock, then the parent check, then the status check, then validation. A missing row and a forbidden row both raise JA403, so existence does not leak. Small side effect: a non-parent briefly holds a row lock before JA403 rolls it back. That is negligible.

- **Important: idempotency keys can collide across occurrences** (migration:156,166). `ledger_entries.idempotency_key` is globally `unique` (ledger.sql:44), and the key is `p_idempotency_key || ':spend'`, taken directly from the caller. If a client reuses a key on a different due occurrence (a static key, or one tied to the form rather than the occurrence), the call fails with a raw 23505 instead of a mapped code. A key matching another family's key would also fail, which hints that the key exists. The row lock plus the `status='due'` check already guarantee exactly once, so the key does not need to be global. Fix: scope it to the occurrence, e.g. `'occurrence:' || v_o.id || ':' || p_idempotency_key || ':spend'`. Or derive it from `v_o.id` alone and treat `p_idempotency_key` as advisory.
- **Minor: test gaps** in handover.test.sql. None of these are in the brief's Step 1 list, but they are cheap to add:
  - no assertion that the `audit_log` row exists after `mark_not_given`;
  - no test that `mark_not_given` on a confirmed row returns `already_handled`;
  - no test that a null or blank key raises 22023;
  - no test that the cash-only case (300, 0) creates exactly one ledger row.
- **Minor:** `lock_occurrence_for_parent` appears in database.types.ts:52-74 even though execute on it is revoked. That is harmless, but app code could try to call it. Consider moving the helper to a private schema so it stays out of the generated types.

**3. ⚠️ Cannot verify from the diff**
- The concurrency behaviour is reasoned from PostgreSQL's locking rules, not tested. pgTAP runs everything in one transaction, so no test covers concurrent double-submit or handover racing not-given.
- I did not re-run the suite. The report's "20 tests pass" claim is unverified.
- I did not check whether any `ledger_entries` triggers or check constraints (`kind`/`source_type` enums) accept `'occurrence'`/`'allowance_in'`. I relied on the claim that the tests pass.

## Task 6 review: parent gifts (e534df1..af113f4)

**1. Spec compliance: ✅**
Everything in the brief and in spec §3 S2–S5, §4.2–4.4 and §11 is in place.
- Table, CHECKs and status default: `supabase/migrations/20261029091517_parent_gifts.sql:11-27`.
- RLS: on, with one select policy `is_parent_of(child_id) or (is_self(child_id) and status='given')` at :52-55. Writes are revoked from authenticated and everything is revoked from anon (:49-50).
- `family_id`: create takes it from the child's approved child membership (:105-113). `profile_id` is unique, so there is a single row. update, cancel and confirm take it from the gift row.
- Missing and forbidden gifts both return JA403 through `lock_gift_for_parent` (:72-74). The helper is revoked from public, anon and authenticated (:349).
- Validation:
  - Create: amount > 0, `give_on >= family_today`, note trimmed with empty stored as null and max 140, all returning 22023 (:115-124).
  - Confirm: cash and saved ≥ 0, sum > 0, sum ≤ 100× planned, computed in numeric (:258-265).
  - `p_idempotency_key` is required non-blank and is not used for ledger keys. Ledger keys are `'parent_gift:'||id||':spend'|':save'` (:276, :286).
- Exactly once: the row is locked `for update`, then status is checked (:251-254). The unique `ledger_entries.idempotency_key` is a second guard. Concurrent confirms serialize on the row lock, and the second gets `already_confirmed`.
- JA409: returned for edit or cancel of a non-pending gift and for confirming a future gift (:247-256).
- Labels: the note, or 'Gift' when there is none. The `-- slice 5: notify(gift_given)` comment is at :298.
- Audit: create, update and cancel write `gift.created`, `gift.updated` and `gift.cancelled`.
- `list_due_for_parent` returns JA403 unless the caller `is_parent_of` the child (:321-323).

**Secrecy hunt (child session): no leak found.**
- `audit_log`: the select policy is approved parents only (`family_core.sql:317-329`), so the child cannot see `gift.*` rows. They also store `has_note`, not the note text.
- `ledger_entries`, `balance` and `list_ledger_history`: nothing is written until handover, and a cancelled gift posts nothing.
- No realtime publication exists in any migration.
- Error-code oracle: every gift function rejects a child or other-family caller with JA403 before any status, date or amount check. So JA409 and 22023 are only reachable by a parent and reveal nothing to the child.
- Generated types expose only the schema, not data.

**2. Quality: Approved.** No Critical or Important issues. Minor:
- **M1, :72 (lock before authz).** `lock_gift_for_parent` takes the `FOR UPDATE` lock before checking `is_parent_of`. A forbidden caller with the gift UUID can briefly block, and could time a locked vs unlocked row. That is a negligible oracle, since UUIDs are unguessable. Fix (optional): run the authz check without the lock first, or add `and public.is_parent_of(g.child_id)` to the locking select so it filters before locking.
- **M2, :73 (authz uses child_id only).** Authz uses `is_parent_of(v_g.child_id)` and ignores `v_g.family_id`. If a child ever moves family (`profile_id` is unique, but a later relink is possible), the new family's parents could edit or confirm the old family's pending gift, and ledger rows would get the old `family_id`. Fix: also require `v_g.family_id = (child's current approved family)`, or cancel pending gifts when membership changes.
- **M3, :164-166 (unchanged past date in update).** `update_gift` accepts an unchanged `give_on` that is already in the past. This bends "dates entered by parents ≥ family today". The reason is sound (otherwise a due gift can't be edited) and the report flags it, but the spec owner should sign off. No test covers this path: add one that edits the amount of a past-due gift and keeps its date.
- **M4, :251-254 (cancelled reported as `already_confirmed`).** `confirm_gift_handover` returns `already_confirmed` for a cancelled gift. This is what the brief asks for. The app layer should still not show "handed over" for it, so note this in the Task 7+ brief.
- **M5, `src/types/database.types.ts` (nullable note).** `p_note` is typed `string`, not `string | null`, which is a codegen limitation. Callers passing null will need a cast or `?? ""`. `""` is harmless, because empty becomes null.

**3. ⚠️ Cannot verify from the diff:**
- That the suite actually passes (66 pgTAP assertions, 339 total claimed). I did not re-run it.
- Real concurrent double submit with two sessions. pgTAP tests it sequentially only, and correctness rests on the row lock.
- Future S6 split-prefill and badge-count functions must stay parent-only, as spec §11 warns about aggregates. They are not in this task.

## Task 7 review (af113f4..328844c)

**1. Spec compliance: ✅**
- Every requested extra is in place:
  - `getLastSplit` is parent-only and picks the newer of confirmed occurrences and given gifts (service.ts:1442-1456, repo :844-886).
  - Every mutation that takes an id reads the row's child, then calls `assertParentOf` before the RPC (`assertParentOfChild`, service.ts:1394).
  - `markNotGiven` returns `"closed"` or `"already_handled"` (repo :1003-1007).
  - The gift note is sent as `note ?? ""` (repo :952, :966).
  - `pauseSchema` rejects a date more than 366 days out with "Pick a date within a year." (schemas.ts:474-486).
  - Schemas are factories that take currency and today; the money copy and the zero-decimal copy match (schemas.ts:405-409).
  - Route helpers in site.ts are functions, and the existing strings are unchanged (diff :78-84).
- Brief items are covered: types, `describeSchedule` and `prefillSplit` (the 333 → 167/166 rounding is right), and every required case in the schema and service tests. Each action calls `refresh()` from `next/cache`, matching `profiles/server/actions.ts`.
- Child safety holds:
  - Child-callable reads (`getSchedule`, `getJars`, `getHistory`) never touch `parent_gifts` or `list_due_for_parent`.
  - `findGift` only runs inside parent mutations. Even there, RLS (`is_self(child_id) and status = 'given'`) hides pending gifts from a child, so a child caller gets FORBIDDEN and no data.
- Layering holds: only the two repositories name tables and RPCs. The client barrel `allowance/index.ts` exports the `"use server"` actions the same way `profiles/index.ts` does.
- The `createAction` contract change is safe. All 11 existing callers (auth, family, profiles) pass schema objects, which are not functions. Validation failures now go through the same `toFailure` path, so results are unchanged. A side benefit is that a non-AppError thrown inside a schema transform now comes back as INTERNAL instead of escaping.

**2. Quality: Approved (Minor only)**
- **Minor:** in service.ts:1568-1570 the allowance branch of `confirmHandover` has no `conflictAs`. A JA409 from `confirm_handover` (for example an occurrence that was already closed) shows the generic CONFLICT copy, while the gift branch gets its own message. Fix: wrap it, e.g. `conflictAs(..., "This allowance was already handled.")`, if the SQL can raise JA409 there.
- **Minor:** in ledger/service.ts:2075-2114, closed occurrences are fetched on their own (newest 50) and are not limited to the time span of the ledger page. When the 100-row page is full, a skipped item can be listed with nothing around it while handed-over allowances from the same period are missing. Fix: drop closed items older than the oldest group kept when `rows.length >= ROW_LIMIT`.
- **Minor:** in ledger/service.ts:2064, `list.pop()` runs on a full page even when the oldest group is a single-row source that was not cut. That costs one item at most, so it is only noted.
- **Minor:** in repo :927, `p_through: args.through as string` hides a mismatch with the generated type. That is acceptable with the comment, but `null as unknown as string` would make the intent clearer, or the type override could live in one place.
- **Minor:** in service.ts:1535, `updateGift` calls `getFamilyContext()`, which repeats `assertUser` and `getFamily` after the action's schema factory already did both. It is harmless but costs extra round-trips. The action could pass `today` in, or the service could call `getFamily` directly.

**3. ⚠️ Cannot verify from the diff**
- I confirmed in the handover migration that `mark_not_given` sets `confirmed_at = now()`, so `listClosedOccurrences` ordering by it works. I could not check that the `confirm_handover` or `confirm_gift_handover` SQL returns exactly `'confirmed'` or `'already_confirmed'`, or what it raises for JA409.
- I did not check that `list_ledger_history` returns `occurred_at` as an ISO string that `Date.parse` reads reliably (PostgREST normally does).
- History `occurredOn` uses the event time, not `due_on`. The brief doesn't say which, and the implementer has flagged it for the UI tasks.
- Typecheck and tests: I did not re-run them, as instructed. The report says `pnpm check` passes.

**Task 8 review (328844c..6d5b8c5)**

**1. Spec compliance: ✅**
- All verbatim copy checks out. Every string in the Global Constraints list appears as written, including the pause explanation and both "Paused until …" variants (`allowance-summary.tsx` PausedNote, `pause-sheet.tsx`, `allowance-form.tsx`, `gift-form.tsx`).
- Setup screen has only amount, frequency and date, plus a note for gifts. "One time" on the create form navigates to `gifts/new` with the amount carried in `?amount=`. "One time" is hidden when changing.
- `ChoiceChips` uses native radios inside a fieldset with a legend, so arrow keys and screen readers work. A visible focus ring is provided via `peer-focus-visible`.
- Stop opens an AlertDialog with "Stop <nickname>'s allowance?" and "Anything already due stays due." The Paused state, Resume, Change and Pause sheet match §5.3.
- Money goes through `formatMoney`, which shows minor units by default.
- Pending gifts appear only on parent pages. `GiftList` and `DueList` are used only in `/parent/children/...`, and the parent home only shows a count.
- Interfaces match the brief (`AllowanceForm`, `GiftForm`, `PauseSheet`, `DueList` linking to `parentHandover`). `GiftList` and `CancelGiftForm` are extras, which is fine.
- The e2e file covers the brief's cases.

**2. Quality: Approved, with Important and Minor items**

*Important*
- `src/app/(app)/parent/page.tsx` (new `dueCounts` block): it makes N calls to `listDue(child.id)`, each running `assertParentOf` plus queries. It also starts only after `getFamily` and `listChildren` resolve, and the `children.length === 0` check comes after the fetch. For ~60 users and a few children this is acceptable. Fix: skip the fetch when `children` is empty, and move the due-count work into a small Suspense child so the family code card isn't blocked.
- `allowance-summary.tsx` `useIdAction`: `run` is typed as returning `{ok:true}|{ok:false,error:{message}}`, and it is called as `resumeAllowanceAction(null, { id })`, i.e. an object rather than FormData. I haven't checked that `createAction` accepts object input and that the action revalidates the page. The e2e resume and stop tests would fail if not, so they are the real check.

*Minor*
- `src/config/site.ts`: `as Route` casts remove typed-route safety for these five helpers. The cast is accepted for now (it avoids typedRoutes friction for dynamic paths), but a single typed helper would be cleaner.
- `gift-form.tsx` `CancelGiftForm`: "Cancel this gift" has no confirmation, unlike Stop. It is a one-click, irreversible-ish action, so consider an AlertDialog. The brief only required one for Stop.
- `allowance-form.tsx`: the `nickname` prop is declared but unused (the doc comment says "for the page title"). Drop it from the form props or use it.
- `alert-dialog.tsx`: `AlertDialogAction` is unused and the Stop button is a plain `Button`. That is fine, but the dialog stays open on error, which is intended.
- `parent/children/[childId]/load-child.ts` and `page-shell.tsx` in `src/app`: these are light composition helpers, not business logic. They sit in the app folder rather than a feature barrel, which is acceptable.
- `pause-sheet.tsx`: when "A date" is chosen the field is `required`. Because the form lacks `noValidate`, the browser blocks an empty date. A blank `through` posted by a crafted request becomes an indefinite pause (the schema allows it), which is harmless.
- `ParentNav` in Suspense (`parent/layout.tsx`) has `fallback={null}`, so the tab bar pops in. It is justified and low impact.
- `playwright.config.ts` and the e2e spec: the `page.getByText("500", {exact:false}).first()` assertions are loose and would pass on any element containing "500". Tighten them to the formatted amount.

**3. ⚠️ Cannot verify from diff**
- That `createAction` accepts non-FormData object input (`resumeAllowanceAction(null, {id})`, `stopAllowanceAction`), and that these actions call `revalidatePath` or `refresh`. The summary and Resume/Stop flow depends on that refresh.
- The `Sheet` and `SheetTrigger render={...}` and AlertDialog `render` props against the installed base-ui version, and `typedRoutes` compile status (the report says `pnpm check` passes; I did not run it).
- The `PageProps<...>` global types and `FieldError id` / `Field` prop passthrough (`value`, `onChange`, `min`) in `@/components/form`.
- The RLS and authz of `listDue`/`listGifts` (Task 7, outside this diff). The pages rely on `assertParentOf` inside the services.
- The e2e tests were not run (they need local Supabase).

1. Spec compliance: ✅ with one caveat. I found no ❌.
- All seven verbatim strings are present and match (`handover-form.tsx` 324, 333, 341, 351, 365, 409; `page.tsx` 46).
- Prefill uses `prefillSplit(item.amountMinor, last)`, so it is the last save share applied to the current amount, and the first handover is all Spend. The e2e checks this: 500.00/0.00 first, then 30.00/20.00 for a 50 gift after a 200/500 split.
- Page rulings are followed. The item is found via `listDue` by kind and id. If it isn't found, the page shows "Already handled" and a link back, never an error and never "handed over" (`page.tsx` 42-57).
- Toasts are exact (239, 398). The idempotency key is made once per mount (232) and submit is disabled until it exists.
- The slider sets the Save share (264-268, 313-320). The shortfall line shows for allowance only, when cash+saved < due (254, 343). Overpay is rejected inline for allowance only and disables submit (255, 260, 363). "Mark as not given" is allowance-only with a confirm dialog (367).
- Caveat: "Same split as last time. Drag or type to change." (341) also shows on the first handover, when there is no previous split. The copy is verbatim as required, so I'm only flagging it.

2. Quality: Approved, with Important and Minor items.

Money arithmetic is sound, so you can drop the float concern.
- Everything goes through `parseMoney`, a digit-string parse to integer minor units with no float. `toMajorString` uses `toFixed(digits)` only to display, and 0 digits returns `String(minor)`.
- `setShare` uses `Math.round(amount*percent/100)` and takes cash as `amount - share`, so the two always sum exactly. With JPY, a 5% step of 100 gives 5, which is fine.
- `prefillSplit` uses BigInt.

**Important**
- `handover-form.tsx` 270-276: when you type a Save value above the due amount, `typeSaved` leaves Spend unchanged. Cash+saved can then exceed the due amount.
  - For allowance, `over` blocks it.
  - For gifts nothing blocks it in the UI. Confirm that the service or schema rejects a gift total that differs from the amount. If it doesn't, add the same inline guard for gifts, or an exact-match guard if gifts must equal the amount.
- 257-259: server `fieldErrors` stay on the inputs after the user edits them, until the next submit.
  - Fix: clear them with a "dirty since submit" flag, or key the state on a counter.

**Minor**
- 281-282: `sliderValue` is the unrounded percent. After typing 33 of 100, the value 33 is off the step of 5, which may snap or look odd in Base UI's controlled slider. Round to the step: `Math.round(x/5)*5`.
- 313-320: the slider range is 0-100 percent, so it can't express a precise minor-unit split. The report admits this. Typing covers it.
- 357: the whole-form error and the dialog error are separate, and nothing stops the main Confirm button while "Mark as not given" is pending. The server's exactly-once guarantee covers it, so this is cosmetic.
- 232: `useEffect(() => setIdempotencyKey(...))` implicitly returns `undefined`, which is fine. Biome may flag the arrow's implicit return in some configs, but the report says `pnpm check` passes.
- `slider.tsx` imports `cn` from `"cn"`. Check that this alias matches the other `ui` files. A mismatch would fail typecheck, so it is probably fine.
- The page does not validate that `kind` is `allowance` or `gift`. This is harmless because it is only used for equality matching.

3. ⚠️ Cannot verify from diff
- Whether `confirmHandoverAction` and the service reject gift totals that differ from the amount (see Important above).
- The `markNotGivenAction(null, { id })` signature and its `"closed"` and `"already"` return values, which are in `server/actions`, outside the diff.
- Whether `Button loading` also sets `disabled`, which affects double-tap protection on the client. Without it, double taps are still deduplicated by the shared idempotency key.
- The 360px layout. The report says it was not visually checked, and the two-column cash/save grid (321) is a risk on narrow screens.
- That `pnpm check` and the e2e run pass. I did not re-run them.

All four findings ADDRESSED. I found no new Critical or Important breakage in the fix diff (child-home.spec.ts excluded). I read the diff and a few source lines but ran no suites.

1. ADDRESSED. `handover-form.tsx` adds a `dirty` flag. It is set true in `setShare`, `typeSaved` and the cash `onChange`, and reset to false at the start of each submit. `fieldErrors` and the `FormError` message are both gated on `!dirty`, so server errors clear on any edit and return only with the next submit result.
2. ADDRESSED. `sliderValue` is now `Math.round(pct / 5) * 5`, clamped to 100. The typed `cash` and `saved` strings are not touched by this, so typed amounts stay exact. The slider only calls `setShare` on a user drag, so a re-derived value cannot overwrite typed amounts.
3. ADDRESSED. The Confirm button is disabled when `notGivenPending` is true. `NotGivenButton` receives `disabled={pending}` from the form and reports its own transition state upward through `onPendingChange`. The trigger gets the `disabled` prop. The `useEffect` callback is `setState`, which is stable, so it does not loop.
4. ADDRESSED. The page passes `hasPrevious={last !== null}`. `getLastSplit` and `prefillSplit` are typed `Split | null`, so the check is sound. The hint copy switches on this flag. The e2e test in `tests/e2e/allowance.spec.ts` now expects the plain hint on the first handover and the "Same split as last time" hint on the gift handover that follows.

Out-of-scope observations (minor):
- `setDirty(false)` runs at the start of submit, so the previous submit's errors stay visible until the new result arrives. This is harmless.
- `hasPrevious` is true even when the previous split totals 0, in which case `prefillSplit` falls back to all cash. The "Same split as last time" hint would then be slightly inaccurate. `getLastSplit` probably never returns a zero-total split, but I did not check.

**1. Spec compliance: ✅** (with two minor notes)

"Skipped" is in `history-list.tsx:389`.
- Amounts use `showMinor: "nonzero"`. The shortfall reads "<given> of <due>" (`history-list.tsx:354-363`). A gift row is 🎁 + note + amount. A skipped row shows the date and "Skipped" with no amount, because `Amount` returns null when `amountMinor` is null.
- The jars take confirmed balances from `getJars`. Due money appears only in the badge.
- Gift secrecy holds. `sumDueOccurrences` reads only `allowance_occurrences` with `status = due`. `listDueForChild` never calls `listDue`, `listDueForParent` or `parent_gifts`. The e2e test also asserts `child.content()` does not contain the note.
- Layering and barrels are fine. The repository alone names the table, the page only composes inside the Suspense'd `ChildHome`, and server exports go through `/server` barrels. There is no console use.
- Minor, spec §5.4 says the card shows "₹500 every Sunday". The card shows the amount (`child-allowance-card.tsx:154`) with "Every Sunday from <date>" on a separate line (`:159`). This is acceptable but not literal.
- Minor, `describeSchedule(frequency, nextDueOn)` yields "Every Sunday from <next date>". This is slightly odd wording, because it is "from" the next occurrence, not the start. It also repeats the date in "Next: <date>" and, when paused, in "Next one: <date>".

**2. Quality: Approved, with Minor items only**
- Minor, `child/page.tsx:73-80`. `getFamilyContext`, `getSchedule`, `getJars`, `getHistory` and `listDueForChild` each run `assertSelfOrParentOf`, so there are about 4-5 repeated authorization lookups per render. This is fine at about 60 users. The `Promise.all` keeps the latency low.
- Minor, `sumDueOccurrences` has no limit. It fetches all due rows and sums them in JS, but the row count is tiny. `count` is returned and never used (`dueTotalMinor` only), which is a small YAGNI.
- Minor, `ledger/index.ts:4` mixes `export *` for history-list with a named export for `Jars`. It works, but it is inconsistent. `export *` on `history-list` is safe because only `HistoryList` is exported.
- Minor, the skipped row's label is "Allowance" in the data (`ledger/server/service.ts:~116`), but the UI correctly shows "Skipped". The date also uses `en-GB` hardcoded, which matches `describe.ts`.
- Minor, `child-home.spec.ts:109`. `history.getByText("Allowance", { exact: true })` could hit more than one element if several allowance rows ever existed. It is fine here because there is one.
- Minor, the e2e test is a single large serial test. A failure midway hides later assertions. The brief asked for one case, so this is acceptable.
- The e2e `playwright.config.ts` project is registered correctly.

**3. ⚠️ Cannot verify from diff**
- The `getHistory` gift `label` is the note, with a fallback when there is no note. I only saw the `allowance` label; I did not read `groupBySource`.

## Final whole-branch review: slice 2 (HEAD f23e9e5)

I read the code and did not re-run any test suites, as you asked. I also checked these SQL contracts across the app/database boundary: confirm_handover and confirm_gift_handover return `already_confirmed` for any row that is no longer pending; mark_not_given returns `already_handled`; create_* derive family_id from the child's membership; change_allowance sets `anchor_on = p_next_on` every time.

### Must fix before merge

1. **`src/lib/money.ts:59` turns a decimal comma into a 100× amount.** EUR is a supported currency (`src/config/site.ts:43`). A parent in a EUR family who types "5,50" gets 55000 (€550.00). For an allowance, the stored amount is 100× too big. For a gift handover, 5,50 against a planned €5.50 is exactly 100×, so it passes the server cap and posts €550 to the ledger, and there is no reversal until slice 3.
   - **Fix:** accept a comma only as a thousands separator (`^\d{1,3}(,\d{3})+(\.\d+)?$`) and return null for anything else. Anchor symbol stripping to the start and end of the string, which also fixes "5₹5" → 55. Add tests for "5,50", "5.", ".5", "1e3" and oversize values.

2. **The cadence line describes the clamped next date instead of the anchor** (`child-allowance-card.tsx:35`, `allowance-summary.tsx:60`). Both call `describeSchedule(frequency, nextDueOn)`. A monthly allowance anchored on 31 Jan shows "Every month on the 28th from 28 Feb" during February. On the child card it also sits right above "Next: 28 Feb", which repeats the date (the T10 flag). Spec §5.4 says "₹500 every Sunday".
   - **Fix:** select `anchor_on` in `findLatestSchedule` (`repository.ts:62`) and add it to `Schedule`. Add `describeCadence(frequency, anchorOn)` with no "from" part ("Every Sunday", "Every month on the 31st"). Render "<amount> every …" plus "Next: <date>". Keep `describeSchedule` for the setup form only.

3. **Changing only the amount silently moves the anchor** (`allowance-form.tsx:55`, together with change_allowance). The edit form pre-fills "Next one on" with `nextDueOn`, and change_allowance always sets `anchor_on = p_next_on, next_index = 0`. Example: a 31st-anchored monthly allowance; in February the parent changes only the amount. From then on it falls on the 28th every month. A 30 Nov quarterly drifts the same way.
   - **Fix:** in change_allowance, keep `anchor_on` and `next_index` when the frequency is unchanged and `p_next_on` equals the current next due date. Alternatively the service sends null for an untouched date. Add a pgTAP case.

4. **A gift that is already due can't be edited or cancelled from the UI, which breaks S5.** `allowance/page.tsx:22` lists only gifts with `giveOn > today`. DueList sends a due gift to the handover page, which has no edit or cancel for gifts (the not-given button is allowance-only). A parent who makes a mistake on a gift for today, say a typo in the amount, is left with a Due badge that never clears unless they hand it over. This also defeats the T6 ruling that update_gift may keep a past date.
   - **Fix:** add a "Change or cancel this gift" link to `routes.parentGiftEdit` on the gift handover page (next to Back, `handover-form.tsx:230`) or on the DueList gift row. Add an e2e case for the cancel-gift dialog (deferred from T8).

5. **The parent never sees jars or history.** Spec §4.4 lists both for the parent, §3 says "the parent sees 'Not given'", and the §8 e2e is "parent hands over → both see jars". No parent page renders `<Jars>` or `<HistoryList>`; only `child/page.tsx` does. After a handover the parent can't check the result, and can't see what they marked as not given.
   - **Fix:** render both on the parent allowance page. `getJars` and `getHistory` already allow parents via `assertSelfOrParentOf`. Give `HistoryList` a `skippedLabel` prop ("Not given" for the parent, "Skipped" for the child).
   - **Or:** get explicit sign-off to descope this and record it in the spec.

6. **The SQL has no cap on the pause date (T3 flag).** At `allowance_schedules.sql:386`, pause_allowance only rejects past dates. A direct RPC call with a date like 9999-12-31 makes the loop at :225 walk roughly 400k weekly periods on every `allowance_next_due_on` call, which runs on every child and parent page render. That family's pages then hit the statement timeout. Zod caps the date, but project rule 9 and the plan's Global Constraints say the database re-checks every input.
   - **Fix:** add `p_through > v_today + 366 → 22023` and a pgTAP case.

### Triage of deferred items

**FLAG FOR FINAL items**
- **T1, parseMoney decimal comma:** must fix (item 1). It causes 100× money errors for EUR families.
- **T3, unbounded paused_through:** must fix (item 6). It's one line of SQL, and the database is the trust boundary.
- **T10, redundant child-card copy:** must fix (item 2). The line is also factually wrong for month-end anchors.

**Other deferred minors**
- **Already resolved:** T2 family_id (create_allowance and create_gift derive it from membership); T5 database.md entries (added in 979cdd3).
- **Moot:** T7 conflictAs on allowance confirm. confirm_handover never raises JA409; a non-due row returns `already_confirmed`.
- **Fold into fix 1:** T1 mid-string symbol, and the missing parse tests.
- **Can wait:** everything else.
  - T1 memoizing the formatters.
  - T2 balance 22023 check and the ledger denial asserts.
  - T3 test for next_on = today.
  - T4 failure-path test, the null guard, and adding sqlerrm to the error record.
  - T4 single transaction holding locks.
  - T5 private schema for the lock helpers.
  - T6 lock taken before authz, relinked family_id, and the past-due edit test.
  - T7 closed rows not bounded to the ledger page, and the family context fetched twice in updateGift.
  - T8 Route casts, the null fallback on ParentNav, and the DueBadge error boundary.
  - T9 the 360px grid.
  - T10 the repeated `assertSelfOrParentOf` calls, the unused count, and the missing paused-until-date e2e.

### Can wait (new minor findings)
- **`tests/e2e/handover-race.spec.ts:145,161,169,184`:** each call signs in its own client inside the `Promise.all`, so the RPC calls start at different times depending on sign-in latency, and the calls may not actually overlap. Sign in all the clients first, then fire the RPCs together. The current assertions are still valid, just weaker evidence of a real race.
- **`handover/[kind]/[id]/page.tsx:19`:** `loadChild` gets the allowance path as `next` rather than the handover path, so after a re-login the parent lands on the allowance page.
- **Edit form while paused with no end date:** the date defaults to today. If today's occurrence already exists, change_allowance's 22023 is shown only as a generic error.
- **Handover form:** typing "5." on the way to "5.50" shows the money error mid-typing.

**Verdict:** not ready to merge. It is ready once items 1–6 are fixed, or once item 5 is explicitly descoped. The money, idempotency and gift-secrecy paths themselves look correct.
