Security audit of slice 2 (908a260..f23e9e5): no Critical, High or Medium findings, and no gift-secrecy leak.

I checked these and found them sound:
- **Gift secrecy.** `parent_gifts` RLS shows the child only rows with status `given`. Every gift RPC goes through `lock_gift_for_parent` or `is_parent_of`, so a child gets JA403 whether the gift is missing, pending or given. `list_due_for_parent` is parent-only. Balance and history only read the ledger, and a gift reaches the ledger only at handover. `audit_log` is readable by parents only (family_core policy). `listDueForChild` sums allowance occurrences only. The child page calls no gift reads. The app returns the same FORBIDDEN "You can't do that." for a child or another family's parent, whether or not the row exists (`assertRole` and `assertParentOf` give the same message). The types file holds only schema. The e2e test asserts the note is absent from the child's HTML.
- **Authz.** Every action goes through `createAction` with Zod and uuid ids. Every service calls `assertParentOf`, or `assertSelfOrParentOf` for reads. Every definer function re-checks `is_parent_of` and uses `set search_path = ''`. Internal helpers and `generate_due_occurrences` are revoked from anon and authenticated. `family_id` always comes from the child's membership or the source row, and `profile_id` is unique, so a child cannot belong to two families.
- **Exactly-once.** Handover takes a row lock, checks status, and uses ledger keys derived from the source row with a unique constraint. The e2e race tests cover this.
- **Secrets.** I grepped the diff and the two `.claude-logs` files for `sb_secret_`, `sb_publishable_`, `sk-ant-`, `eyJ` and connection strings, with no hits. The only email outside example.com is a GitHub noreply address. The e2e admin client is limited by `assertLocalUrl`. No `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`, and login redirects use `safeNextPath`.

**Low**

1. `supabase/migrations/20261029091517_parent_gifts.sql:52` — after handover, a child querying PostgREST directly with their own session can read the whole given-gift row. That includes the planned `amount_minor`, `give_on`, `created_at` and `updated_at`, so the child can see "planned 20, got 15" and when the gift was set up. This does not break S3, which only covers before handover, but it is more than the UI shows. Fix (pick one):
   - Add column-level grants on the table, e.g. `revoke select … ; grant select (id, child_id, status, note, cash_minor, saved_minor, given_at) …`.
   - Or drop the child branch of the policy and rely on the ledger rows alone.
   - Or record it in the spec as accepted.

2. `supabase/migrations/20261029085518_allowance_schedules.sql:386` and `:225` — `pause_allowance` only stops past dates. The one-year cap exists only in Zod (`schemas.ts` `MAX_PAUSE_DAYS`). A parent calling the RPC directly with `p_through = '9999-12-31'` makes `allowance_next_due_on` loop about 400k times on every child home render. It hurts only their own family. Fix: add `or p_through > v_today + 366` to the 22023 check. Optionally put the same bound on `p_first_on`, `p_next_on` and `p_give_on`.

3. `20261029085518_allowance_schedules.sql:14,56`, `20261029091517_parent_gifts.sql:15`, `20261029084533_ledger.sql:91` — money columns have no upper bound in the database; only `parseMoney` caps at `Number.isSafeInteger`. A direct RPC with amounts near the bigint maximum can overflow `balance()`'s `::bigint` cast (error 22003, so the child home shows INTERNAL). Above 2^53, `Number()` in the repositories also loses precision. Fix: add a check constraint such as `amount_minor <= 100000000000` on `allowance_schedules`, `allowance_occurrences`, `parent_gifts` and `ledger_entries`. Also check that `p_cash_minor` and `p_saved_minor` stay within it in both confirm functions.

4. `20261029091517_parent_gifts.sql:72` (same pattern in `handover.sql:17` and `allowance_schedules.sql:188`) — the lock helpers take `FOR UPDATE` before the authz check, so anyone signed in can briefly lock any row whose UUID they know. The transaction aborts at once and UUIDs can't be guessed, so this is negligible. It was already deferred in Task 6 and needs no action now. Optional fix: read without the lock, check `is_parent_of`, then lock and re-read.

Verdict: OK to merge from a security and child-safety view. Lows 2 and 3 are cheap hardening for a follow-up, and Low 1 needs a product decision.

No Critical findings. Two Important issues (#1, #2) are small fixes and should land before merge; the rest can wait until slice 3. I reviewed by reading the code only, as asked: no tests run, no DB, no file changes.

**Critical**
- None.

**Important**
1. **Pause dates have no upper bound, so a loop can run forever.** `allowance_schedules.sql:386` accepts any `p_through` of today or later, and `allowance_schedules.sql:225` steps period by period up to it. Weekly paused to 9999-12-31 is about 416k iterations per read. Paused to the maximum date it runs past the 8s `authenticated` statement_timeout, or hits "date out of range". Because this function serves the read path, both the parent and child screens fail on every load while the pause lasts. The same unbounded date is also accepted for `p_first_on` (`:275`), `p_next_on` (`:340`) and gift `give_on` (`parent_gifts.sql:120`). Fix: cap every date argument at family today plus 1–2 years (raise 22023). Then replace the loops at `allowance_schedules.sql:225`, `:436` and `allowance_generation.sql:52` with one closed-form helper, `allowance_first_index_after(anchor, freq, date)`. For weekly it is `floor((d - anchor)/7)+1`; for months, take the month difference and correct by at most one.
2. **A closed or cancelled row reports success.** `handover.sql:44-45` returns `already_confirmed` for a `closed` (not given) occurrence. `parent_gifts.sql:249-250` does the same for a `cancelled` gift. So if parent A marks not given or cancels, and parent B then confirms, B's UI shows "handed over" but nothing reaches the ledger. Fix: return a result per status (`already_confirmed` / `already_closed` / `cancelled`), or raise JA409 for the terminal non-given states.
3. **There is no per-child lock for ledger writes, which slice 3 needs.** Purchases and withdrawals must check the balance and then insert. With nothing to serialise on, two concurrent spends can push a jar negative. Fix: settle the convention now and apply it in `confirm_handover` and `confirm_gift_handover` too. Take `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended('ledger:'||child_id,0))` first, then the source row's `for update`. Document the order in `docs/database.md` so slices 3–4 cannot invert it. Today's order (occurrence, then insert) is deadlock-free only because nothing locks the child yet.
4. **Some ledger invariants are not enforced by the schema** (`ledger.sql:34-55`). Add:
   - a check tying kind to direction (`*_in`/`grant_in` ⇒ `in`; `purchase`/`*_out` ⇒ `out`);
   - `check ((kind='reversal') = (reverses_entry_id is not null))`;
   - a unique index on `reverses_entry_id` in place of the plain one at `:60`, so an entry cannot be reversed twice.

   This costs nothing now. After slice 3 undo ships it needs a data audit first. Name the constraints so later migrations can drop and re-add the kind list cleanly.
5. **Cron failures can leave no trace in `job_runs`** (`allowance_generation.sql:18`, `:75-79`). The whole run is one transaction, so a timeout, a cancel, or an error outside the per-schedule block rolls back the `job_runs` row itself. Only `cron.job_run_details` keeps any trace. Nothing alerts on `status='error'` or on a missing recent `ok` run. Also, the `/api/cron/[job]` fallback the spec relies on (§10) does not exist under `src/app/api`. Fix:
   - add `set local statement_timeout` in the job;
   - have the existing `/api/health` route, or a small admin-only check, flag when there has been no `ok` run in more than 2h;
   - either build the fallback route or remove it from the spec.

**Minor**
6. **The history query cannot use the ledger index.** `ledger.sql:57-58` is `(child_id, mode, jar, occurred_at desc)`. `list_ledger_history` (`ledger.sql:128-131`) reads across both jars and orders by `occurred_at, created_at`, so every call sorts all of the child's rows. Also, with no cursor, anything older than 200 rows can never be reached. Fix: index `(child_id, mode, occurred_at desc, created_at desc, id) include (jar, direction, amount_minor)`, which also gives `balance()` an index-only scan, and add `p_before_occurred_at` / `p_before_id` keyset paging before purchases add volume.
7. **Occurrence state is not checked by the table.** In `allowance_occurrences` (`allowance_schedules.sql:50-65`), add `check (status <> 'confirmed' or (cash_minor is not null and saved_minor is not null and cash_minor+saved_minor+shortfall_minor = amount_minor and confirmed_at is not null))`, matching what gifts already have. `mark_not_given` reuses `confirmed_by/at` for the close; consider `closed_by/at` or renaming to `handled_by/at`.
8. **The due list has no matching index.** `list_due_for_parent` would use a partial index `allowance_occurrences (child_id, due_on) where status='due'`; `allowance_schedules.sql:72` is not partial.
9. **Overflow gives the wrong error code.** At `handover.sql:50-51`, `p_cash_minor + p_saved_minor` can overflow bigint and raise 22003 instead of 22023. Cast to numeric, as gifts already do.
10. **The idempotency key is checked but never stored** (`handover.sql:52`, `parent_gifts.sql:263`). The spec (§4.3, "same key returns original result") expects it stored. Either store it and return the original result, or drop the parameter in the slice 3 signature changes.
11. **"Next due" can be stale for an active schedule.** `allowance_schedules.sql:229-230` returns a past date when the generator has lagged, and it lags on purpose for a removed child (`allowance_generation.sql:28-36`). If that child is approved again, every missed period is backfilled as due. Consider applying the same skip-to-today rule that resume uses.
12. **`balance()` treats a bad argument as an empty jar.** It does not validate `p_jar`/`p_mode` (`ledger.sql:84`), so an invalid value returns 0 silently.
13. **A child can see what was planned versus given.** The RLS policy at `parent_gifts.sql:55` shows a given gift's planned `amount_minor` as well as `cash_minor`/`saved_minor`. Expose only what was handed over, for example through a read function.
14. **Two misleading comments in the cron setup.** The comment at `allowance_generation.sql:96-97` says to enable the extension on the hosted project before release, but the migration creates it itself (pg_cron is listed as available on hosted Supabase). The skip branch at `:106` uses `raise notice`; prefer `raise warning`.

**Checked and fine**
- **Locks:** I found no deadlock path. Parent functions lock one row each, the generator uses `skip locked`, and the occurrence update never touches FK columns.
- **Exactly-once:** holds through `for update`, the read-committed re-check after the wait, and the unique ledger keys derived from the source row.
- **Cron scheduling:** `cron.schedule` by job name updates the existing job instead of adding a second one, and two overlapping runs cannot double-generate.
- **Period math:**
  - stepping from the anchor clamps month-ends correctly (Jan 31 → Feb 28 → Mar 31);
  - family timezone handling is correct;
  - the :05 hourly run makes periods due at most about 1h after local midnight.
- **Grants:** the revokes cover Supabase's default grants, and the types are regenerated.

**Verdict:** Merge after fixing #1 and #2. Do #3–#5 before slice 3 starts.

Verdict per item
- A1 ADDRESSED: src/lib/money.ts:58-87. Symbol is stripped only at the start or end, the THOUSANDS regex gates commas, the symbol Map is memoised, and the safe-integer guard is in. All listed cases are tested in money.test.ts.
- A2 ADDRESSED: describe.ts:51-84 adds describeCadence and cadenceAfterAmount. anchorOn is selected in repository.ts:617. The summary and the child card render "<amount> every …" plus "Next: …". describeSchedule is now used only by the form.
- A3 ADDRESSED: allowance_schedules.sql:347-367 re-reads next_index after catch-up, and v_keep_anchor is correct. The pgTAP case is date-independent.
- A4 ADDRESSED: handover/[kind]/[id]/page.tsx:52-59 adds the link. The edit page uses listPendingGifts, which has no date filter. e2e covers the dialog copy.
- A5 ADDRESSED: allowance/page.tsx:47-71 adds a nested Suspense ChildMoney with showMinor="always" and skippedLabel="Not given". e2e checks the jars and "Not given".
- A6 ADDRESSED: handover page.tsx:217-220. The `next` value always starts with /parent/children/, so it cannot become an open redirect.
- A7 ADDRESSED: handover-race.spec.ts signs in all clients first (parentClients(n)), then fires one Promise.all.
- B1 ADDRESSED: all five functions cap dates at today + 366. Zod uses withinYear with the copy "Pick a date within a year." updateGiftSchema now takes today. pgTAP covers each function, including the 366 edge.
- B2 ADDRESSED: four named `*_amount_minor_max` checks. Both confirm functions bound cash and saved and sum them as numeric. pgTAP sends a bigint max and gets 22023.
- B3 ADDRESSED: parent_gifts.sql:1271-1278. The policy is now parents only. Docs updated in domain.md #17, database.md and architecture.md.
- B4 ADDRESSED: handover.sql:1207-1210 and parent_gifts.sql:1367-1368. Order is source row FOR UPDATE, then validation, then the advisory lock, then inserts. The lock order is documented.
- B5 ADDRESSED: ledger.sql:50-58 adds ledger_kind_direction and ledger_reversal_link, and the partial unique index replaces the plain one.
- B6 ADDRESSED: allowance_schedules.sql adds allowance_occurrences_confirmed_split and the partial `where status='due'` index.
- B7 ADDRESSED, with the deviation accepted: null-today guard, `left(sqlerrm,200)` in job_runs, fixed comment, `raise warning`. The timeout sits in the cron command (allowance_generation.sql:1174-1178). This is sound: since PG13 a simple-query multi-statement string applies the timeout per statement, so a value set by statement 1 governs statement 2. The cost: a manual `select generate_due_occurrences()` runs with no cap. That is acceptable.
- C1 ADDRESSED, with a nit: the slice 2 spec §11 gained the new line, but the line above it still says "The `/api/cron/[job]` fallback stands." The two lines contradict each other; delete the old one.
- C2 ADDRESSED: database.md now covers the lock order, the constraints, the date caps, keep-anchor and the cron command.

New Critical/Important breakage: none found.
- Gift secrecy after B3 holds. The child's history and jars come from the security-definer list_ledger_history and balance functions, which read ledger_entries only. Every parent_gifts read in the app is on a parent-only path: listGifts, findGift and findLatestGiftSplit, used by the handover and edit pages and the parent services. No child path loses data.
- B4 lock order is safe. The only ledger writers are the two confirm functions. mark_not_given, the generator and the schedule functions write no ledger rows and take no advisory lock, so nothing locks in the reverse order.
- B5 and B6 do not reject any legitimate write. confirm_handover writes allowance_in/in, confirm_gift_handover writes parent_gift_in/in, and no reverses_entry_id is set. confirmed_split always holds because confirm_handover sets shortfall = amount - cash - saved and confirmed_at = now(). mark_not_given sets 'closed', which the check does not constrain.

Minor A3 edge (not blocking):
- Where: allowance_schedules.sql:360. Applies to a schedule that is paused with a through-date.
- Scenario: the form prefills allowance_next_due_on, which is the first period after the pause, not period next_index. An amount-only change therefore fails the keep-anchor test and re-anchors.
- Effect: harmless unless that date is a clamped month-end, for example a 31st schedule whose post-pause date is 28 Feb. Then the 31st cadence drifts to the 28th.
- Fix: compare p_next_on against the same pause-skipping index that allowance_next_due_on uses, and keep the anchor with that index.

Out of scope:
- A1 rejects lakh grouping ("1,00,000"). The user gets a friendly error and no wrong amount is stored, so it is safe. INR users will hit it; a follow-up could also accept `^\d{1,2}(,\d{2})*,\d{3}`.
- The parent's empty History card reuses the child's copy, "Your money story starts here."
- A6 has no dedicated test.

Ready to merge: yes. The C1 contradictory line and the paused A3 edge are optional cleanups.

I found nothing that must be fixed. The diff matches the brief: routes, nav, Home, the child page, the slimmed manage page, the Allowance tab, the history page, redirects and e2e updates. I read the code and did not re-run any suites.

Checked and fine:
- **Auth:** every new page goes through `requireRole` or `loadChild`. `listDue` and `listGifts` call `assertParentOf`, and `listGifts` returns pending gifts only.
- **No pending-gift leak:** no child-app files changed, so no pending-gift data can reach a child page.
- **Barrels:** they are respected, and no server-only API is pulled into a client file.
- **Home doesn't block:** the h1 is static. Per-child promises start in `ParentHome` and each card and "Needs you" waits inside its own `<Suspense>`. Every promise is awaited, so none is left unhandled.
- **Redirects:**
  - handover, not given, already handled, gift save/cancel and the edit-gift "already handled" link all go to `routes.parentChild`;
  - allowance setup goes to the child page and a change goes back to the manage page, as the brief says.
- **Accessibility:** table headers are scoped, the action column has a screen-reader label, nav items are labelled and the child-card link has a name. Tap targets are 36 px (`sm`) only inside parent tables and 44 px (`lg`/`min-h-11`) elsewhere.
- **E2E tests:** the existing tests were not weakened, only re-routed. The family code is now read from Settings and the PIN reset happens via Settings. The new specs cover Needs you → handover → child page → "All caught up." and Allowance tab → Manage. "See all" is covered by a unit test.

**Should fix**
1. `src/features/family/components/child-switcher.tsx` and `family-code-card.tsx`, exported at `src/features/family/index.ts:6,9`: both are now dead (only their own files mention them). Settings shows the code as a plain `<dd>` at `src/app/(app)/parent/settings/page.tsx:41-42` with no copy button. Fix: delete both files and their barrel exports. Or, if the copy button is worth keeping, render `FamilyCodeCard` in Settings instead of the `<dd>`.
2. `src/app/(app)/parent/page.tsx:522-530`: flattening and sorting due items across children (`perChild.flat().sort(...)`) is a domain rule living in `app/`, and it has no test. Fix: add a pure `mergeDue(perChild)` to `src/features/allowance` (beside `describe.ts`), unit-test oldest-first order across children, and have the page call it.
3. Per-child fan-out: `src/app/(app)/parent/page.tsx:408-413` makes 3 service calls per child, and `src/app/(app)/parent/allowance/page.tsx:25-30` makes 2. Each call runs its own uncached `isParentOf` RPC on top of the data query, so about 6 round-trips per child on Home. That's fine at about 60 users and it doesn't block the shell. If it grows, wrap `isParentOf` in React `cache` keyed by childId. The Allowance tab waits for all children before showing anything; acceptable, but worth knowing.

**Nits**
- `src/features/allowance/components/allowance-overview.tsx:702`: a stopped schedule reads "No allowance running", but the brief says "No allowance yet". The Status column already says "Stopped", so either use "No allowance yet" or keep it deliberately.
- `src/features/allowance/components/needs-you.tsx:1032`: the count badge has no screen-reader word, so the region is announced as "Needs you 2". Add `<span className="sr-only"> waiting</span>`, as `DueBadge` does elsewhere.
- `src/features/allowance/components/child-overview-card.tsx:887`: the card is clickable as a whole, but the focus ring only circles the nickname. Consider `focus-visible:after:ring-2` on the `::after` overlay so the ring outlines the whole card.
- `src/app/(app)/parent/children/[childId]/page.tsx:337`: the fallback title "Your child" flashes before the nickname loads. The brief allows it, but a title-less skeleton would avoid the flash.
- `src/app/(app)/parent/children/[childId]/handover/[kind]/[id]/page.tsx:28,40` and the gift new/edit pages still show the "Your family" eyebrow instead of a top back link to the child. The form's Back and Cancel buttons do go to the right place, so it's only a consistency gap with the new `PageShell` `back` prop.

Verdict: approve. Fixing the two dead components and moving the due merge into the feature are worth doing before merge but don't block it.
